14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\cdots,

where
H^{2}(X,{\cal O})
is one-dimensional.
The
connected analytic Brauer group
is defined as the ‘one-dimensional’ subgroup

H^{2}(X,{\cal O}^{\ast})^{0}\coloneqq H^{2}(X,{\cal O})/\operatorname{im}(H^{2}(X,\mathbb{Z})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2}(X,{\cal O}))\subset H^{2}(X,{\cal O}^{\ast}).

Furthermore, since the connected analytic Brauer group is divisible, the algebraic Brauer group
{\rm Br}(X)\subset H^{2}(X,{\cal O}^{\ast})
maps onto
H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{\text{tors}}
.
Thus, the intersection

(2.1)

{\rm Br}(X)^{0}\coloneqq{\rm Br}(X)\cap H^{2}(X,{\cal O}^{\ast})^{0}\subset{\rm Br}(X)

is a subgroup of finite index

|H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{\text{tors}}|
. In particular, any
\alpha\in{\rm Br}(X)
of order coprime to the order of

H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{\text{tors}}
is necessarily contained in
{\rm Br}(X)^{0}
.

Remark 2.1
.

In the literature one finds the following information about
H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{\text{tors}}
for
the four known deformation types of compact hyperkähler manifolds:

(i)

The cohomology of the Hilbert scheme
S^{[n]}
of a K3 surface is torsion free, cf.
[
44
,
56
]
. In fact,
H^{3}(S^{[n]},\mathbb{Z})=0
.

(ii)

The cohomology of the generalised Kummer variety
K_{2}(A)
of dimension four is torsion free, cf.
[
36
]
. In fact,
H^{3}(K_{2}(A),\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{\oplus 4}
. This is expected to hold for all
K_{n}(A)
.
2
2

2

Torsion freeness was originally claimed in all dimensions, but a problem with the proof was pointed out by Totaro, see the Corrigendum.

(iii)

The odd Betti numbers of varieties of OG6-type are trivial, cf.
[
46
]
. It is expected that
H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})=0
.

(iv)

The odd Betti numbers of varieties of OG10-type are trivial, cf.
[
15
]
. It is expected that
H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})=0
.

So it seems, that at the moment no compact hyperkähler manifold is known that would admit non-trivial torsion classes in
H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})
. However, there is no a priori reason why
{\rm Br}(X)^{0}={\rm Br}(X)
should always hold.

By definition of a Lagrangian fibration, the restriction maps
H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{2})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{0}(A_{t},\Omega^{2}_{A_{t}})
and their complex conjugates

H^{2}(X,{\cal O})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2}(A,{\cal O})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2}(A_{t},{\cal O})

to the smooth fibres
A_{t}

are trivial. In particular, the image of

{\rm Br}(X)^{0}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm Br}(A)
is contained in
{\rm Br}_{1}(A)
. Altogether this proves that restriction
to the open subset
A\subset X
defines a map

{\rm Br}(X)^{0}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm Br}_{1}(A)^{0},

so that Corollary
1.7
applies to all classes in
{\rm Br}(X)^{0}
and thus to
all classes of order coprime to
|H^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{\text{tors}}|
.
Since in the Leray spectral sequence
H^{2}(X,{\cal O}_{X}^{\ast})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{1}(B,R^{1}f_{\ast}({\cal O}_{X}^{\ast}))
maps

H^{2}(X,{\cal O}_{X}^{\ast})^{0}
into
H^{1}(B,(R^{1}f_{\ast}({\cal O}_{X}^{\ast}))^{0})
(use continuity), one obtains the map

{\rm Br}(X)^{0}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{1}(B,(R^{1}f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m})^{0}),

so that the discussion of the preceding sections applies.

2.2.
Line bundles on the compactified dual abelian scheme

We continue to consider a Lagrangian fibration
h\colon X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}
of a projective hyperkähler manifold
X
. We fix in addition an ample line bundle
{\cal L}
on
X
and also use
it as a polarisation of the smooth part
f\colon A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
, which may not have a section.

The dual abelian scheme
\check{A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B

parametrises numerically trivial line bundles on the fibres

A_{t}
. Since these fibres are integral, any line bundle on one of them, considered as a sheaf on the ambient projective
variety
X
, is stable with respect to any polarisation. Hence,
\check{A}
can be viewed as an open subset
of the locally projective moduli space of all sheaves that are semi-stable with respect to the polarisation
{\cal L}
, see
[
28
,
55
]
for the construction and further references.
Let us denote by
{\cal M}
the irreducible component of this moduli space that contains
\check{A}
. So,
{\cal M}
is now a projective variety for which the support map
{\cal M}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}
extends the twisted Picard scheme:

\textstyle{\check{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{\cap}
\textstyle{\cap}
\textstyle{{\cal M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}.}

The existence of the support map requires the morphism
h\colon X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}
to be flat which
is in fact equivalent to the smoothness of the base, cf.
[
31
, Rem. 1.18]
.

Of course, the projective moduli space
{\cal M}
comes with a (non-unique) ample line
bundle. To prove Theorem
0.3
with an unspecified integer
N_{X}
, this is all that is needed. However, if one wants to be more specific, e.g. as in Corollaries
1.7
and
1.12
, then we need control over the degree of the ample line bundles
on the fibres
\check{A}_{t}\subset\check{A}\subset{\cal M}
. This should be considered
as a technical issue and the reader is encouraged to skip the proof of the next result
at first reading. Its proof will occupy the rest of the section.

Proposition 2.2
.

There exists a line bundle
M
on
{\cal M}
such that its restriction
M_{t}\coloneqq M_{\check{A}_{t}}

satisfies
\chi(\check{A}_{t},M_{t})=m_{1}\cdot\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})^{m_{2}}
for certain integers

m_{1}
and
m_{2}
. Moreover, the coefficient
m_{1}
can be chosen coprime
to any given integer
r
.

The basic idea is to compare the polarisations of
{\cal M}
given by its description as a moduli space with the polarisation of the dual abelian
scheme
\check{A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
discussed before. We start by recalling both settings.

\bullet
Let us first consider again the dual abelian scheme
\check{A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
. If
A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
admits
a section, then a universal Poincaré bundle
{\cal P}
on
\check{A}\times_{B}A
exists and
it is unique if appropriately normalised. However, to describe it explicitly on a fibre
A_{t}
,
it is common to use a polarisation
{\cal L}
on
A
, its restriction
{\cal L}_{t}\coloneqq{\cal L}_{A_{t}}
on
A_{t}
,
and the induced morphism
\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}}\colon A_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\check{A}_{t}
.
Then, cf.
[
49
, §​ 13]
:

(2.2)

(\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}}\times{\rm id})^{\ast}{\cal P}|_{\check{A}_{t}\times A_{t}}\simeq m^{\ast}{\cal L}_{{t}}\otimes p_{1}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{{t}}^{-1}\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{{t}}^{-1}.

Here,
m\colon A_{t}\times A_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces A_{t}
is the multiplication and
p_{1},p_{2}

denote the two projections. Used as a Fourier–Mukai kernel, the Poincaré bundle
{\cal P}

induces an equivalence
\Phi_{\cal P}\colon{\rm D}^{\rm b}(A)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.375pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm D}^{\rm b}(\check{A})
, which
we have used before to define a polarisation
L\coloneqq\check{{\cal L}\,\,}\!\!
on
\check{A}

as the dual of the determinant of
\Phi_{\cal P}({\cal L})
. To control the degree of the restriction

L_{t}\coloneqq L|_{\check{A}_{t}}
, we express the
pull-back
\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}}^{\ast}L_{t}
using a result of Mukai
[
47
, Prop. 3.11]
.
Fibrewise it says

(2.3)

\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}}^{\ast}\Phi_{\cal P}({\cal L}_{t})\simeq H^{0}(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})\otimes{\cal L}^{\ast}_{t}\text{ and hence }\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}}^{\ast}L_{t}\simeq{\cal L}_{t}^{\chi({\cal L}_{t})}

which together with
\deg(\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}})=\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})^{2}
implies
\chi(\check{A}_{t},L_{t})=\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})^{g-1}
, see also
[
8
]
.

Note that in the following, we will not use the existence
of a zero section of
A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
nor the existence of
{\cal P}
on
\check{A}\times_{B}A
. All computations will be done on the geometric fibres
A_{t}
and
\check{A}_{t}
where the
Poincaré bundle
{\cal P}_{t}
on
\check{A}_{t}\times A_{t}
always exists.

\bullet
Alternatively, we can treat
\check{A}
as (an open subset of) a moduli space of stable sheaves on
A
or
X
and as such it is constructed as a GIT quotient of some Quot-scheme, see
[
28
,
55
]
for details and references concerning the construction of moduli spaces of
semi-stable sheaves. More
precisely, we consider all sheaves parametrised by
\check{A}
, so numerically trivial line bundles
P

on the fibres
A_{t}
, as quotients
({\cal L}_{t}^{-N})^{\oplus\chi_{N}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces P
, where
\chi_{N}\coloneqq\chi({\cal L}_{t}^{N}\otimes P)=\chi({\cal L}_{t}^{N})=N^{g}\cdot\chi({\cal L}_{t})
and
N
is chosen sufficiently big. At this point in the discussion,
N>2
is sufficient, but we might need higher powers later for the compactification
{\cal M}
.

Then there is an open subset
{\mathcal{R}}_{B}\subset\text{Quot}_{B}
of the relative Quot-scheme

\text{Quot}_{B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
of quotients
of
({\cal L}_{t}^{-N})^{\oplus\chi_{N}}
parametrising only line bundles on the fibres
A_{t}
such that

\check{A}
is a good quotient by the natural
\text{PGL}(\chi_{N})
-action

(2.4)

\pi\colon{\mathcal{R}}_{B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\check{A}={\mathcal{R}}_{B}/\!/\,\text{PGL}(\chi_{N}).

The Quot-scheme representing the Quot-functor comes with a universal quotient sheaf

\tilde{\cal P}
on
\text{Quot}_{B}\times_{B}A
which is used to identify relatively ample line bundles

(2.5)

\tilde{M}_{\ell}\coloneqq\det p_{1\ast}(\tilde{\cal P}\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}{\cal L}^{\ell}),

\ell\gg 0
, on
\text{Quot}_{B}
, see
[
28
, Ch. 4.3]
. The GIT quotient
(
2.4
) is in fact formed with respect to the linearisation given by
\tilde{M}_{\ell}
and, therefore,
\tilde{M}_{\ell}
descends to an ample line bundles
M_{\ell}
on
\check{A}
.

While
L=\check{{\cal L}\,\,}\!\!
on
\check{A}
is easier to construct, it is
the line bundles
M_{\ell}
that naturally extend to line bundles on
{\cal M}
under the inclusion
\check{A}\subset{\cal M}
and we will need to have control over their degree on the fibres. This is achieved by comparing the line bundles
M_{\ell}
to
L
and the first step is comparing
the restrictions
\tilde{\cal P}_{t}\coloneqq\tilde{\cal P}|_{\text{Quot}_{t}\times A_{t}}
and
{\cal P}_{t}\coloneqq{\cal P}|_{\check{A}_{t}\times A_{t}}
.

Choose a basis of
H^{0}(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t}^{N})
and compose it with the evaluation map
to produce a surjection
{\cal O}_{A_{t}}^{\oplus\chi_{N}}\simeq H^{0}(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t}^{N})\otimes{\cal O}_{A_{t}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\cal L}_{t}^{N}
. Then pulling back under the multiplication

m\colon A_{t}\times A_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces A_{t}
, tensoring with
p_{2}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{-N}
, and applying (
2.2
) yields a surjection

({\cal O}\boxtimes{\cal L}_{t}^{-N})^{\oplus\chi_{N}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}\times{\rm id})^{\ast}({\cal P}_{t})\otimes p_{1}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{N}

on
A_{t}\times A_{t}
, which in turn defines a classifying morphism

\tilde{\psi}\colon A_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\text{Quot}_{t}
with

(\tilde{\psi}\times{\rm id})^{\ast}\tilde{\cal P}_{t}\simeq(\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}\times{\rm id})^{\ast}({\cal P}_{t})\otimes p_{1}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{N}

on
A_{t}\times A_{t}
.
Note that by construction, the composition
\psi\coloneqq\pi_{t}\circ\psi\colon A_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\text{Quot}_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\check{A}_{t}
is simply
\varphi_{{\cal L}^{N}_{t}}
.

With this we can now numerically identify the ample line bundle
M_{\ell}
on
\check{A}

on the fibres.

Lemma 2.3
.

For
\ell=N\cdot\ell^{\prime}\gg 0
, the line bundle
M_{\ell t}
on
\check{A}_{t}
is ample
with

\chi(\check{A}_{t},M_{\ell t})=N^{g^{2}-g}\cdot\ell^{\prime g^{2}-g}\cdot(\ell^{\prime}-1)^{g}\cdot\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})^{g-1}.

For the application we have in mind the precise form of the right hand side
is of no importance. The only thing that
matters is that first
N\gg 0
and then
\ell^{\prime}\gg 0
can be chosen such that any
r
coprime
to
\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})
will also be coprime to
\chi(\check{A}_{t},M_{\ell t})
.

Proof.

By the above and using the shorthand
\chi_{\ell}\coloneqq\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell})
, one has

\displaystyle\varphi_{{\cal L}^{N}_{t}}^{\ast}(M_{\ell t})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\psi^{\ast}(M_{\ell t})\simeq\tilde{\psi}^{\ast}(\tilde{M}_{\ell t})\simeq\det\tilde{\psi}^{\ast}(p_{1\ast}(\tilde{\cal P}_{t}\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\det p_{1\ast}\left((\tilde{\psi}\times{\rm id})^{\ast}\tilde{\cal P}_{t}\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\det p_{1\ast}\left((\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}\times{\rm id})^{\ast}{\cal P}_{t}\otimes p_{1}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{N}\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\det\left(p_{1\ast}\left((\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}\times{\rm id})^{\ast}{\cal P}_{t}\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}\right)\otimes{\cal L}_{t}^{N}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\det\left(\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}^{\ast}\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell})\otimes{\cal L}_{t}^{N}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}^{\ast}\det\left(\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell})\right)\otimes{\cal L}_{t}^{N\cdot\chi_{\ell}}.

For
\ell=N\cdot\ell^{\prime}
we write
\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}}\colon A_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\check{A}_{t}
as the composition
(\ell^{\prime}\cdot~)\circ\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}\colon A_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\check{A}_{t}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\check{A}_{t}

and use (
2.3
) to compute

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}^{-\ell\cdot\chi_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\det(({\cal L}_{t}^{-\ell})^{\oplus\chi_{\ell}})\simeq\det\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}}^{\ast}\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\det\varphi^{\ast}_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}(\ell^{\prime}\cdot)^{\ast}\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell})\simeq\varphi^{\ast}_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}((\ell^{\prime}\cdot)^{\ast}\det\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\varphi^{\ast}_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}\left((\det\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}))^{\ell^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}+1)/2}\otimes(-1)^{\ast}(\det\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell}))^{\ell^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}-1)/2})\right),

where we use the standard formula for
(n\cdot)^{\ast}L
of a line bundle
L
on an abelian variety.
One could speculate that
\det\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell})
is in fact symmetric, in which
case one would have

(2.6)

{\cal L}_{t}^{-\ell\cdot\chi_{\ell}}\simeq\varphi^{\ast}_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}\left(\det\Phi_{{\cal P}_{t}}({\cal L}_{t}^{\ell})\right)^{\ell^{\prime 2}},

but in any case we know that (
2.6
) is always true up to numerical equivalence. This then shows that
\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}^{\ast}(M_{\ell t})^{\ell^{\prime 2}}
is numerically equivalent to
{\cal L}_{t}^{\ell^{g+1}\cdot\chi_{1}\cdot(\ell^{\prime}-1)}
, which is enough to conclude

\displaystyle\chi(\check{A}_{t},M_{\ell t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\deg(\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}})\cdot\ell^{\prime 2g})^{-1}\cdot\chi(A_{t},\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}}^{\ast}(M_{\ell t})^{\ell^{\prime 2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\deg(\varphi_{{\cal L}_{t}^{N}})\cdot\ell^{\prime 2g})^{-1}\cdot(\ell^{g+1}\cdot\chi_{1}\cdot(\ell^{\prime}-1))^{g}\cdot\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell^{g^{2}-g}\cdot(\ell^{\prime}-1)^{g}\cdot\chi(A_{t},{\cal L}_{t})^{g-1}

and thus proving the assertion of the lemma.
∎

We can now complete the proof of Proposition
2.2
. The moduli space
{\cal M}

is constructed as a GIT quotient of some open subset of semi-stable points

{\mathcal{R}}\subset\text{Quot}

{\mathcal{R}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\cal M}={\mathcal{R}}/\!/\,\text{PGL}(\chi_{N})

extending (
2.4
) that described
\check{A}
as a GIT-quotient. The universal quotient
\tilde{\cal P}
on
\text{Quot}_{B}\times_{B}A
extends to a universal quotient
on
\text{Quot}\times_{\mathbb{P}}X
and the formula (
2.5
) also describes
ample line bundles on
{\cal M}
, again denoted
M_{\ell}
. We now apply the
previous lemma with
m_{1}=\ell^{g^{2}-g}\cdot(\ell^{\prime}-1)^{g}=N^{g^{2}-g}\cdot\ell^{\prime g^{2}-g}\cdot(\ell^{\prime}-1)^{g}
and
m_{2}=g-1
combined with the observation that this
m_{1}
can obviously assumed to be coprime to any given
r
with
N
and then
\ell^{\prime}
are chosen sufficiently large.∎

2.3.
Vanishing of the obstruction class

We are now taking the next step towards extending the obstruction
o(\alpha,L^{r})\in{\rm Br}(B)
from
B
to
\mathbb{P}
with the ultimate goal to show its triviality by using the triviality of
{\rm Br}(\mathbb{P})
.
Here,
L
denotes the restriction of the ample line bundle
M
on the compactification
\check{A}\subset{\cal M}
as in Proposition
2.2
, so depending on the period
r={\rm per}(\alpha)
.
There are again similarities between the discussion in this section and Abasheva’s results
[
1
, §​ 3.0.3]
. The main difference is that Abasheva twists an actual hyperkähler manifold while we twist a moduli space
{\cal M}
of sheaves on a hyperkähler manifold
with
{\cal M}
usually not even be smooth.

Recall that the obstruction class
o(\alpha,L^{r})
was obtained as the image of a certain class
\alpha^{\prime}
under
the boundary map
\delta\colon H^{1}(B,\underline{\check{A}}[r])\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2}(B,\mathbb{G}_{m})
induced
by the short exact sequence (
1.7
),
which, using the identification
\underline{\check{A}}[r]=R^{1}f_{\ast}\mu_{r}
,
can alternatively be written as

(2.7)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{{\rm Aut}}((\check{A},L^{r})/B)[r]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{1}f_{\ast}\mu_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\,.}

We then proceed in two steps:

(i)

The short exact sequence (
2.7
) extends from
B
to a
short exact sequence on
\mathbb{P}
:

(2.8)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{1}h_{\ast}\mu_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\,.}

In fact, for technical reasons the quotient sheaf
R^{1}h_{\ast}\mu_{r}
will be replaced by a
certain subsheaf
{\cal K}_{r}\subset R^{1}h_{\ast}\mu_{r}
which still restricts to

R^{1}f_{\ast}\mu_{r}
.

(ii)

The class
\alpha^{\prime}\in H^{1}(B,R^{1}f_{\ast}\mu_{r})
is the restriction of a class in
H^{1}(\mathbb{P},R^{1}h_{\ast}\mu_{r})
(or rather in
H^{1}(\mathbb{P},{\cal K}_{r})
).

Let us begin with (i) for which we will make use of a slightly bigger open subset

\check{A}\subset P\subset{\cal M},

parametrising only stable sheaves on
X
that are line bundles on the fibres
X_{t}

which are in addition numerically trivial on each component.
Note that then
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}
is a group scheme and clearly surjective.
Moreover, tensor product on the fibres defines an action
P\times_{\mathbb{P}}{\cal M}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\cal M}

which we use to prove the following result.

Lemma 2.4
.

There exists a natural map

(2.9)

R^{1}h_{\ast}\mu_{r}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\underline{{\rm Aut}}({\cal M}/\mathbb{P})

extending
R^{1}f_{\ast}\mu_{r}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(R^{1}f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m})^{0}\simeq\underline{{\rm Aut}}^{0}(\check{A}/B)
, cf.
(
1.1
)
.

Proof.

The sheaf

Lemma 2.5
.

The image of

)
is contained in the image of

Proof.

Ideally one would like to prove that already the image of (

.

Using the arguments and the notation in the proof of the previous lemma, it would be enough to show that tensor product with an

.
For this, observe that the pull-back

of

under
K\otimes(~~)\lx@nobreakspace\colon{\cal M}_{U}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\cal M}_{U}
and the original line bundle

are isomorphic over the pre-image of

, see the proof of Proposition

.

In order to conclude that they

could be continuously deformed (through analytic line bundles) to the trivial line bundle, this would be immediate. By
means of the exponential sequence, we know that the obstruction to do this in the analytic category is the first Chern class

, but by definition this
is guaranteed for all local sections of the subsheaf

.
∎

This allows us to pull-back (

) to obtain a short exact

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\cal K}_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The restriction to

Let us now turn to (ii), so we want to show that the class

is in the image of the restriction map

Clearly, then the obstruction class

is in the image of the restriction map

Recall that

For this we go back to the exponential sequence in §​
